This Train Offers a Front-Row Seat to One of Colorado’s Greatest Natural Wonders

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“Locomotives all have their own attitudes and personalities,” says Adam Clawson, the 24-year-old engineer at the helm of Royal Gorge Route Railroad, as our train winds along the Arkansas River. “They are their own living, breathing machines, and you have to learn their personalities.”

It’s a warm Saturday afternoon in October, and Clawson is at the controls of a locomotive he knows well: “Black Betty,” a jet-black, 3,000-horsepower diesel-electric unit that runs along a 12-mile stretch of tracks in Southern Colorado. Clawson is a third-generation railroader, following in the footsteps of his grandfather and great-grandfather, who both worked along this same route.

The Royal Gorge Route Railroad winds along the Arkansas River in Colorado.

As he eases the rumbling locomotive through the gorge, he offers a running commentary on the geology and history unfolding outside the window. Millions of years ago, he says, as the Rockies rose above the plains, the Arkansas River began carving its ancient path through the emerging peaks. Over time, the water wore away the hard granite, cutting a narrow, V-shaped gorge that plunges as deep as 1,250 feet in places.

The result is the Royal Gorge, a geological wonder nicknamed the “Grand Canyon of the Arkansas River.” Visitors can experience the dramatic 10-mile-long chasm from several perspectives: From above, while gazing down from the Royal Gorge Bridge, the tallest suspension bridge in the nation (or for more adventurous travelers, while rock climbing a 5-hour via ferrata); and from below, while paddling the Arkansas’s roaring rapids on a whitewater raft. But for travelers looking to sit back and savor the scenery passing by, there's no better viewpoint than the Royal Gorge Route Railroad's open-air car, standing face-to-face with the staggering canyon walls.

The writer's view aboard the Royal Gorge Route Railroad

This train line is as historic as it is scenic. Though the tourist train didn't launch until 1998, the route itself has been here for nearly 150 years—but it was close to never being built in the first place. In the late 1870s, a pair of competing railroad companies—the Denver & Rio Grande and the Santa Fe—duked it out for the chance to build tracks through this narrow canyon. Their two-year battle, which involved everything from court injunctions to sabotage to gun-slinging hired guards, became known as the “Royal Gorge War.” Eventually, the two companies signed a treaty and the Denver & Rio Grande prevailed, building tracks that eventually reached the lucrative mining hub of Leadville.

Decades later, the train still threads through the same Old West landscape, but the ride is decidedly more comfortable. Today passengers can choose from three classes: Vista Dome, which offers nearly unobstructed views of the gorge from glass-enclosed dome cars; Deluxe Class in the vintage dining car, where four-top tables with cushioned booth-style seats and a bar are nestled against the large windows; and Coach Class, with four-person table seating and the option to order food and drink from the casual grill menu and bar. On select dates, there are also First Class dining tickets available for lunch and dinner, with elevated menus and wine pairings. Regardless of where you sit, all ticket holders have access to the open-air car, which is standing-room only. With Clawson or one of his fellow engineers at the controls, all travelers have to do is sit back and let the scenery unfold.

The journey begins roughly an hour south of Colorado Springs in Cañon City at a historic depot originally completed in 1914. A few miles later, a dilapidated pipeline comes into view, running along the canyon wall just above the river. Completed in 1910, this eight-mile-long, mostly wooden structure provided water to Cañon City until 1973. As the train passes under the Royal Gorge Bridge, Clawson encourages me to open the cab window, lean back, and look up. The wind whips my hair as I stare at the bridge some 1,000 feet overhead. It’s 18 feet wide, according to Clawson, but from this vantage point, it looks like a thin wire that’s been strung across a gaping expanse.

As the train works its way through the river gorge, water rushes past and red rock walls tower on both sides.

Next, the train squeezes through the gorge’s narrowest and deepest section, the towering red granite walls growing taller and more imposing as they tighten around the tracks. Then we chug over the famous “Hanging Bridge,” which was an engineering marvel when it was completed in 1879—and remains one even today. At this point, the chasm narrows to just 30 feet wide, creating a bottleneck that required a little ingenuity to get a train through. Engineer C. Shallor Smith came up with a brilliant solution: He designed a 175-foot-long bridge that’s suspended above the rushing water by A-shaped supports anchored to the canyon wall.

Soon after, Clawson spots a pair of plastic kayaks hauled out on a small beach on the other side of the waterway. “We might get mooned,” he warns, referencing how paddlers traditionally greet the train when it passes. But when we spot the kayakers standing on the bank a few minutes later, they simply smile and wave. Clawson blares the train’s horn in response.

Eventually, the train reaches its western terminus, the town of Parkdale. Clawson hits the brakes and we jump down onto the tracks. Standing in front of the train up close, I’m struck by just how imposing she is. Thinking back to Clawson’s personality comment, I imagine her as fiercely independent—a self-assured, no-nonsense type of gal who’s not easily pushed around.

We clamber back into the cab and the train heads in reverse, retracing the route toward Cañon City. I relax back into my seat, letting the engine’s vibrations and the rhythmic drone of the wheels lull me into a pleasant daze. Outside, the rocky surfaces of the gorge’s walls alternate between light and shadow while the river glints in the midday sun. High overhead, I spot a turkey vulture circling on a thermal, its wings arching in a shallow V as it glides without flapping.

Clawson follows my gaze. “You can’t make this up,” he says. “Somebody might type into AI, ‘Take this picture of me and make it look like I’m in paradise.’ [But] all I gotta do is step outside."

As the most discerning, up-to-the-minute voice in all things travel, Condé Nast Traveler is the global citizen’s bible and muse, offering both inspiration and vital intel. We understand that time is the greatest luxury, which is why Condé Nast Traveler mines its network of experts and influencers so that you never waste a meal, a drink, or a hotel stay wherever you are in the world.

NYC Votes to Cut Construction Red Tape in Bid to Spur More Homebuilding

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New York City voters overwhelmingly approved a plan to overhaul the city's notoriously complicated construction permitting process Tuesday, handing Mayor Zohran Mamdani a victory in his effort to accelerate housing development.

Question 4, one of five ballot measures approved in the Nov. 3 election, received roughly 68% support, according to preliminary election results.

The measure promises to simplify construction by bringing 40 types of permits and approvals currently handled by 18 separate government offices and agencies into one centralized digital system.

"New Yorkers voted for a government that moves as fast as they do," Mamdani said in a statement Tuesday night, adding that the reforms would "directly address the affordability crisis and help New Yorkers stay in the city they love."

However, the new system isn't required to launch until 2030. Even when it does, faster permitting will address only one of the many obstacles making housing so difficult and expensive to build in New York.

"No single reform will be a silver bullet for housing supply and affordability, especially in a city like New York," explains Jake Krimmel, senior economist at Realtor.com®. "Instead, each pro-supply policy change is a baby step in the right direction. And these marginal gains will add up."

The Big Apple needs approximately 700,000 additional homes over the next decade to meet existing and future demand, according to an August 2026 draft assessment from the city's housing and planning departments.

Yet developers filed plans for just 8,064 multifamily units in the second quarter of 2026, in a stunning 52% decline from the previous quarter, according to the Real Estate Board of New York.

That amounts to less than half the 17,500 new homes the city needs to add each quarter—and they're only proposed units, not completed homes.

Centralizing permits could help close that gap by cutting down the time and cost builders are forced to spend navigating city bureaucracy.

"Streamlining approvals can make a huge difference," Krimmel says. "It might not be the flashiest reform, but when it comes to new construction, time is money."

And permitting takes a very, very long time in New York City.

Of the nearly five years it takes to build a typical 20-unit apartment building in the Big Apple, roughly 1.5 years are spent securing permits alone, according to a September 2026 study examining construction timelines across 60 U.S. cities.

Now compare that to similar projects in Raleigh, NC, or Phoenix, AZ, where it takes roughly two years from application to completion.

"In a city like New York City with high land acquisition costs and a complex regulatory environment, predictability in timelines and financing can be critical for ensuring new construction can pencil," Krimmel says.

And research suggests those time savings could translate into more homes.

Cutting approval times by 25% could have increased multifamily housing production in Los Angeles, CA by 12.7% between 2010 and 2022, according to a 2026 study published in the Journal of Urban Economics.

When researchers accounted for additional development that faster approvals might encourage, the estimated increase reached 23.7%.

The question for New Yorkers, though, is when those reforms might translate into more housing options and lower rents. The median asking rent hit $3,785 in October—up 4.5% from a year ago.

Under the new rules, applicants would have one digital portal to submit documents, track applications, and obtain approvals, rather than navigating separate systems across multiple agencies.

But streamlining applications doesn't necessarily mean they'll be reviewed any faster.

In fact, the average time required to approve applications through the Department of Buildings' existing digital portal climbed from 8.3 days in 2020 to 20.3 days in 2025, according to a July report from New York State Comptroller Thomas DiNapoli.

And its hardly the only challenge. Krimmel says the overhaul won't address other barriers to construction, including zoning restrictions and the complexity of financing multifamily developments.

Still, he sees the reform as one step toward untangling a much larger problem.

"In the same way that a complex web of red tape and veto points is extremely good at creating barriers to new construction, real solutions will require the city untangles that web bit by bit," he says.

With the new system potentially four years away, the first test will be whether it actually shortens permitting timelines. Any corresponding increase in housing construction—and relief for renters—could take considerably longer.

As Mamdani acknowledged Tuesday night, "Now comes the work of turning these votes into results."

Allaire Conte is a senior advice writer covering real estate and personal finance trends. She previously served as deputy editor of home services at CNN Underscored Money and was a lead writer at Orchard, where she simplified complex real estate topics for everyday readers. She holds an MFA in Nonfiction Writing from Columbia University and a BFA in Writing, Literature, and Publishing from Emerson College. When she’s not writing about homeownership hurdles and housing market shifts, she’s biking around Brooklyn or baking cakes for her friends.

Lens-Como 1-9, le pagelle: che cosa ha cucinato Fabregas? Nico Paz totale, triplo Douvikas

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Scegliete voi la console, ma al Bollaert-Delelis è sembrato assistere ad una partita di calcio virtuale, ma alla fine gli enfant terribles di Fabregas impartiscono una lezione al Lens in casa sua: 9-1 il risultato finale, per una figuraccia senza precedenti per i Sang et Or, oltre che per il record di otto reti mai realizzate da una squadra nella storia della Champions League. Un tripudio dei pochi ma quanto sono stati fortunati i tifosi lariani in trasferta per assistere ai primi tre punti fuori casa in Europa dei biancoblù e il quarto posto agguantato con 9 punti per i playoff di Champions praticamente ipotecati.

LE PAGELLE DEL COMO (a cura di Yvonne Alessandro) Butez 6 - Incolpevole sulla prelibatezza di Aguilar, si gode da una postazione privilegiata la storia del Como in Champions League e un 9-1 epocale.

Couto 6,5 - Alle volte si prende dei rischi inutili, ma non si può bocciare il brasiliano in una notte del genere. Peccato per quell'assist sprecato da situazione vantaggiosa.

Ramon 7,5 - Fa paura. Negli anticipi si divora Ivanovic e Thauvin a turno in un sol boccone, quando accompagna l’azione è tambureggiante. Ed è un centrale, anche se non ci meravigliamo più. Difensore che fa gola alla Premier e al Real Madrid per una ragione.

Kempf 7,5 - Un gladiatore. Annulla Thorgan Hazard, si concede uno dei tanti gol della serata ma è pur sempre il suo primo sigillo in carriera in Champions League. Minuti di stima e ringraziamento in Champions.

Valle 7 - L'assist in corsa per Douvikas sarebbe un invito a notte per qualsiasi centravanti. Trova un po' di gloria anche lui.

Milla 7 - L’esperto sui calci piazzati per un motivo. Con il destro fatato trova la testa di Perrone che si trasforma in assist per l'1-0 di Douvikas, ma che palla mette per il pokerissimo di Kempf? Perrone 7 - Un errore in costruzione, un’altra sbavatura nel giro di nemmeno tre minuti per un avvio in affanno. Si dà una ripulita e vale metà del primo gol di Douvikas. Va a un passo dalla rete personale nel tentativo di infierire sul Lens.

Da Cunha 7,5 - Si aggiunge alla fiera del gol con un mancino chirurgico in fondo alla porta, ma si mangia l'erba per la voracità. Dal 58' Liberali 8,5 - È davvero entrato dalla panchina? Pronti, via e si inventa prima una rete da beach-soccer, di punta a sorprende Risser. I due assist per Baturina e Papaccioli sono classe pura.

Nico Paz 8,5 - Che cosa abbiamo visto al Bollaert-Delelis? Qualcosa che va oltre la definizione di top player. Una zurda velenosa impegna Risser, come avesse la calamita al piede si divincola in spazi inesistenti e per poco non trova il golazo. Ma non tarda ad arrivare e si inventa anche due assist per una notte che non lo farà dormire questa notte dall'euforia. Dal 58' Baturina 7,5 - Aveva gli occhi della tigre quando è entrato in campo. A tutti i costi voleva iscriversi nel tabellino dei marcatori e ce l'ha fatta: chapeau.

Animale d’area di rigore, si cala anche in assist-man per Nico Paz. Dal 61' Papaccioli 7,5 - Sogno o son desto? No, ragazzo, è tutto vero: a 18 anni e alla prima apparizione in Champions League hai firmato il tuo primo gol assoluto. Ma è lecito avere un po' di stupore.

Allenatore: Fabregas 10 - Meriterebbe il premio di MVP. Non sarà sceso in campo, ma quello che ha creato questo allenatore in poco più di due anni e mezzo è qualcosa di impensabile. In casa del Lens autografa un risultato da record, 9 gol con 7 marcatori diversi. Playoff virtualmente blindati, alle prime apparizioni assolute in Champions League. Che cosa ha cucinato lo chef.

LE PAGELLE DEL LENS (a cura di Matteo Selli) Risser 4 - Serata storica in negativo per lui, che probabilmente vorrebbe essere in qualsiasi altro posto tranne che a difendere i pali del Lens stasera. Trova anche un paio di parate che evitano altri gol.

Antonio 4.5 - Soffre tremendamente la qualità dell’attacco comasco. Destro di piede, schierato braccetto di sinistra, sbaglia tantissimo anche in uscita palla. Salva un gol sulla linea che avrebbe reso il passivo ancora più pesante.

Baidoo 4 - Perde sistematicamente Douvikas, che fa quello che vuole nella metà campo avversaria. Il greco è in stato di grazia, ma lui gli rende la vita facile.

Aguilar 5 - Segna un gol bellissimo, al volo, che prova a rendere meno amara una serata storica, in negativo, per i suoi.

Udol 4 - Dalla sua parte arrivano i maggiori pericoli, senza che lui possa avere le armi per contrastarli.

Cuisance 4.5 - Anche lui totalmente in affanno, prova un paio di conclusioni da lontano, ma la sua partita è tutta lì.

Haidara 4.5 - Partita difficilissima per tutti, ma per il centrocampo forse ancora di più. Perde anche la pazienza e con un paio di interventi duri lo dimostra. Dal 70’ Bulatovic sv.

Thauvin 4.5 - Dovrebbe essere il leader tecnico della squadra, ma il condizionale in questo caso è d’obbligo. Surclassato dagli avversari, non riesce mai ad inventare qualcosa. Dal 70 Kadile sv.

Skóraś 5 - Gara complicata la sua, prova qualche sortita e qualche cross, senza fortuna. In fase difensiva è surclassato. Dal 46’ Abdulhamid 4.5 - Entra in un secondo tempo sportivamente drammatico per i suoi, e può pochissimo.

Ivanović 5.5 - Ha una buona occasione nel primo tempo quando Butez lo ferma bene in uscita, ma per il resto è isolato contro l’attenta difesa avversaria. Dal 46’ Mesloub 5.5 - Prova a dare la scossa con le sue giocate. Non ha successo, ma sicuramente è uno dei più vivi dei suoi.

Hazard 5 - Prova qualche sortita, ma senza fortuna, in una serata nefasta per i suoi.

Andrea Bowers Salutes the Climate Change Activists of the 1980s at Fondazione Sandretto Re Rebaudengo

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Andrea Bowers’ solo exhibition “La Ragnatela” at the Fondazione Sandretto Re Rebaudengo in Turin, Italy, is unintentionally, aptly timed, given its focus on the politics of climate change: Last year, Italian Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni hit pause on solar panels on farmland, calling it a “threat to our food sovereignty.”

Bowers’ show spotlights the Italian anti-nuclear feminist movement and women’s camp La Ragnatela—“the spiderweb”, in Italian—that launched in 1983 in Comiso, Sicily, to protest the installation of US nuclear cruise missiles at the NATO base in Comiso. The show is a mini-retrospective composed of Bowers’ political activist work which was in dialogue with the movement. There are works by Bowers that she created in 2003, straight out of graduate school, as well as pieces produced in 2013, 2022, 2023 and four newly created artworks for the exhibition. Related Articles Melting Ron DeSantis Statue Makes a Point About Climate Change in Florida Collective Climate Action Implemented by Los Angeles Arts Institutions

Much of La Ragnatela’s history has been forgotten, so Bowers worked with curator Bernardo Follini to find and rebuild the network of Italian ecofeminists. Follini found three women, who are now in their 80s and 90s, who contributed documents and clippings to the exhibition.

“The social engagement part of my practice is the least known about or documented because the art world is more focused on objects, but this is what I’m most proud of,” said Bowers during a preview in Turin, dressed in head-to-toe black with her Botticelli-esque hair flowing freely.

The show opens with Defense of Necessity (2003), a quilt that nods to women’s traditional crafts, and it’s one of the first pieces that Bowers created as a political artist. On close inspection of the quilt, there are sowings of spiderwebs, a symbol for the Women’s Pentagon Action, an American grassroots ecofeminist and anti-nuclear movement that was simultaneously taking place in Italy by way of La Ragnatela and in England with the Greenham Common Women’s Peace Encampment. Defense of Necessity is held up as a fence in the show. “The fence is the site often where nonviolence is questioned whether it’s becoming violence. Activists who are involved in nonviolence are always saying, if we cut that fence, does it become violence? Or is this called an offense of necessity, which is an argument that smaller injustices, or you break small laws to protect the world from larger injustices,” explained Bowers. “It’s a legal term and because it has the word ‘fence’ in it, it’s a play on words and it’s poetic.”

On the other side of the fence are four vitrine cases, two of which feature a timeline of the La Ragnatela movement. The others display letter exchanges between the women in Comiso and their comrades in England, who eventually traveled to Comiso to help them protest. There are photographs of some of the women who were arrested for protesting, along with a letter from one of the women, who was detained for a long time and put on trial, during which she wore a T-shirt that says “question authority.” Bowers took the tagline and added it to Neon Spiderweb #3 (2026), a neon spiderweb with the words sitting on the outer string of a web—the artist also made three other neon works with such slogans: “A better end of the world is possible,” “Return our future” and “To be free from fear.” The political slogans of hope pour over into Political Ribbons (2026), brightly colored sheets of silky ribbons that repeat the mottos on the neon signs or feature new sayings: “People over profits,” “Outlaw women are the only hope for earth’s survival” and “Widen the web to break the nuclear chain.” Visitors are urged to take ribbons from the wall with them when they visit the show. Bowers explains that it’s a “simple way for people to take a position.” It’s a methodology that she’s used before, adding that to this day, she still gets a thrill out of seeing them in the wild or online.

Bowers’ pieces in the show command attention no matter their size. Her graphite on paper works: Diabloblockade, Diablo Nuclear Power Plant, Abalone Alliance, 198 (2003) and Women’s Pentagon Action, 1981, Detail of Woven Web Around Pentagon (2003) are hyperrealistic. the artist has recreated photographs that were given to her by her mother’s friends who were part of the Californian eco-group Mother for Peace. In Cardboard (2026), a larger-than-life cardboard with an “empowered Italian woman” facing her back to the audience holding a flag that says “Sun and wind,” the artist taught herself how to use an iPad to draw the image that’s been projected onto a thick cardboard.

The show at the Fondazione Sandretto Re Rebaudengo is sort of a homecoming for Bowers, as she last showed at the foundation in 1998 as part of a group show titled “Guarene 98.” Some 28 years later, her pieces from the Chandeliers of Interconnectedness franchise grace the halls of the space as visitors make their way to other exhibitions by Nan Goldin or Joseph Yaeger. The show, however, feels cut short. Perhaps Bowers has more to say than can fit the space she was given. (It would have been a better idea to give her two galleries to really demonstrate two decades of hard passion for the environment.)

Still, she managed to make a mark with Neon Opland (2026), another neon sign that imitates a doodle from a La Ragnatela poster of a woman kicking a missile with the words “No ai missile” written beneath. It’s somewhat phallic, a comical touch made more apparent by teenage couple who were flirting on a bench opposite its neon lights.

Political Ad Revenue Breaks Record For Sinclair, Lifting It To Better-Than-Expected Q3 Results

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sinclair cashed in on political advertising during the third quarter even before the “blue tsunami” officially reached shore. The broadcaster rode a 55% gain in the category compared with the previous midterm election in 2022 to post better-than-expected financial results.

The company said it sold $136 million in political ads during the July-to-September period, with the spending hitting an all-time record of $455 million in 2026, ahead even of the 2024 presidential haul of $405 million. The prior high for a midterm cycle was $333 million in 2022.

Total revenue increased 16% from the prior-year quarter, hitting $893 million. Adjusted EBITDA more than doubled to $213 million, with earnings per share of 60 cents swinging to the black after a 2-cents-per-share loss a year earlier. Related Stories News Snap Swings To Profit As SPECS Set To Roll Out; Stock Takes A Hit News Killer Q3 For UFC Helps TKO Beat Wall Street Earnings Forecasts

CEO Chris Ripley said more quarterly results are yet to be reported by rival companies, but it appeared that local TV stations as a sector maintained market share levels from prior political cycles. With live sports continuing to pace the overall linear landscape, some political campaigns opted for national broadcast buys rather than local stations, given the comparative expense profiles. That created the odd spectacle of East Coast sports fans being served PAC-funded spots backing Senate candidates in Texas or Iowa. Watch on Deadline

In 2015 and 2016, as Donald Trump was rising to dominance, Sinclair played a key role in his unforeseen triumph over Hillary Clinton. At the time, Sinclair was also known for more expressly right-of-center programming and “must-run” editorials. A decade later, and in the wake of Democrats’ stinging rebuke of Trump in the midterms, the climate has shifted. Still, Sinclair remains one of the primary local players, though it is now No. 2 station behind market leader Nexstar.

Rob Weisbord, COO and president of local media, told Wall Street analysts on the company’s earnings call that the political haul this year “bodes well for the upcoming 2028 election cycle, which will be here before we know it. Notably, 2028 will present a dual open primary for both parties, for the presidential nominations. So we expect early and aggressive spending as both parties will want to introduce themselves nationally. While our footprint is well positioned throughout many battleground states, we also believe that the entire broadcast sector did well during the election cycle.”

He added that management commentary at rival companies as well as industry forecasts provide evidence “of local broadcast television being a key delivery platform of political advertising.”

Como da record in Champions League: nessuno aveva mai segnato 9 gol in trasferta

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Una notte da leggenda per il Como, che travolge il Lens con un clamoroso 1-9 e riscrive, in un colpo solo, la propria storia e quella della Champions League. Prima di questa sera, infatti, nessuna squadra era mai riuscita a segnare nove reti in trasferta nella competizione. Dal 1992 a oggi, cinque formazioni si erano spinte fino a quota sette: il Marsiglia nel 2010, lo Shakhtar Donetsk nel 2014, il Liverpool nel 2017, il Bayern Monaco nel 2019 e il PSG nel 2025. Soltanto il Bayern era riuscito a fare meglio, arrivando a otto gol nella storica trasferta di Barcellona del 2020. Un primato che sembrava destinato a resistere, almeno fino alla notte del Bollaert-Delelis, dove la banda di Fabregas ha alzato ulteriormente l'asticella, spingendosi dove nessuno era mai arrivato prima.

Con il 9-1 rifilato al Lens, il Como entra anche in un club esclusivissimo: quello delle squadre capaci di chiudere una partita di Champions League con otto reti di scarto. Un'impresa riuscita, prima dei lariani, soltanto a Liverpool e Real Madrid. I Reds ci erano riusciti nel 2007, travolgendo il Besiktas con un 8-0 ad Anfield, mentre i Blancos avevano replicato nel 2015, rifilando lo stesso risultato al Malmo al Bernabeu. Da questa sera, però, anche la squadra di Fabregas può vantare un posto in questa ristretta élite.

Con i dieci gol segnati allo Bollaert-Delelis, Lens-Como diventa anche la terza partita con più reti nella storia della Champions League assieme ai dieci gol sommati che ci furono in Barcellona-Bayern Monaco del 14 agosto 2020 (2-8) e alle spalle degli undici gol in Monaco-Deportivo La Coruña, terminata 8-3 il 5 novembre 2003 e dei dodici che si videro a Signal Iduna Park il 22 novembre 2016 tra il Borussia Dortmund e il Legia Varsavia, che finì 8-4.

Mckenna Grace Invoked District 12 When Talking About Her Love Life (I Get It Considering Who Hunger Games Fans Ship Her With)

It can be tricky for co-stars who have great chemistry to get away from the shipping culture online, particularly when it’s already got a massive book following. So it makes sense that their love lives are going to come up more the bigger the fandom is, and right now, thanks to Sunrise on the Reaping, one of the actors can’t seem to stop talking about her relationship… and she’s not complaining. Mckenna Grace invoked District 12 when talking about her love life and I get it, considering who Hunger Games fans ship her with.

When Sunrise on the Reaping hit the 2026 movie schedule, I knew it wouldn’t be long before ships started sailing, especially after getting the first reactions to the movie. Grace plays District 12 tribute Maysilee Donner (along with her twin Merrilee). So naturally, she spends a lot of time with Joseph Zada who plays young Haymitch, and even though she’s happily dating a different co-star, Mason Thames, fans are still shipping them. The actor made sure to shout out her partner in an interview discussing all things Hunger Games with Cosmopolitan.

While dishing about manifesting her Hunger Games role and being "Team Peeta," she assured fans that she’s never been happier. She has been dating Thames since the two took their Regretting You romance from the screen into real-life. The couple went public earlier in the year on Valentine’s Day, and have been going strong ever since. Grace shares:

Your girl is happy. I’m feeling very lovey-dovey. I have somebody I can be a complete nerd with. I got my best friend, and so I don’t see the point in not loving loudly.

Grace is going all in on "loving loudly," and I’m loving how similar it is to her dedication as a fangirl. After all she dressed like Jennifer Lawrence for the prequel’s press tour. According to Grace, she doesn’t see the point in keeping her feelings to herself:

There are moments that are just for you and your person. But I don’t see the point in not celebrating who you’re in love with. A lot of people’s hesitation around this stems from the idea that if you post about someone a bunch and then you break up, that’s embarrassing. But why would I even think about that? I’m just going to focus on the present.

It can be stressful having a relationship in the public eye, but the actor seems to be handling with grace. Though I didn’t expect her to invoke District 12 when bringing up her dedication to Thames. In hindsight, given how much of a fan girl she is of Peeta Mellark, I should have. Additionally, in what can only be described as a Tom Holland move, Thames was often hanging out around the cornucopia with her while filming:

It’s a terrifying thing in a relationship to surrender yourself to that thought of, I’m just going to be at my most vulnerable and trust you, and this part of my life is fully in your hands. But I don’t see the point in building a relationship based on fear. I am the biggest fan of the person I’m with, so why wouldn’t I be like, ‘This is my person. I am so stoked I have this’? We’re always on each other’s sets anyway. He’s like an honorary member of District 12 at this point.

The shout out to Thames’ visits could have to do with die hard fans shipping her IRL with co-star Zada. It definitely feels like her polite way of reminding people, because she's well aware of what the fans want to see. During the press tour, the co-stars were asked what they thought.

The two quickly laughed it off and described it as “pretty weird.” For them, it goes beyond the two already dating other people. While Grace is outright about her relationship with Thames, Zada goes a more private route with his non-actor girlfriend. Truthfully, it has to do with the fact that they view each other like family thanks to long hours on the Hunger Games prequel. Check out this exchange between them during an interview with Refinery29 Australia:

However, this hasn’t stopped the two from calling out their own favorite ships amongst their castmates. From Zada and Ben Wang to Grace and Laura Marcus, there’s plenty to choose from, but for Grace she’s team ‘Maydove.’ In fact, she informed Zada that she’s “coming for [his] girl.” (Though I’m surprised Grace didn’t shout out Peeta and Katnis, especially since they reprised their roles in the movie. We all know about Grace's fan edits.)

Regardless of ship preference, it’s nice to see the co-stars having fun with the shipping culture, and not taking anything to heart. Besides, Grace is clearly thriving in her "lovey dovey" era with her very own Peeta and I love that for her.

Porto-Napoli 4-1, le pagelle: tutte le colpe ad Allegri? Era la partita di Gabri Veiga

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Porto-Napoli 4-1, al Do Dragao arriva una doccia gelida per i partenopei, che crollano nel secondo tempo dopo aver terminato in parità la frazione inaugurale di gioco. A segno nel primo tempo Bednarek e McTominay, nella ripresa i gol di Veiga, Kiwior e Samu su rigore.

Diogo Costa 6 - Impegnato in tre occasioni, tutte nella stessa azione. Bravo su Politano, si supera su Hojlund ma non può far nulla su McTominay. Per il resto viene chiamato in causa soltanto con qualche timido tentativo dalla distanza.

Alberto Costa 6,5 - Per almeno settanta minuti è una spina nel fianco per la retroguardia del Napoli, ara la corsia di destra creando la superiorità numerica con Pepé. Grande gara per l'ex Juventus.

Bednarek 7 - Domina nel gioco aereo, in entrambe le fasi. Dopo undici minuti porta avanti il Porto con un imperioso colpo di testa, nella ripresa prova il bis ma la traversa gli nega la gioia di una serata indimenticabile di suo con il primo gol in Champions League.

Kiwior 7 - Non ha grandi problemi nel contenere Hojlund in una sfida dove il Napoli si fa vedere pochissimo davanti, poi nel finale emula Bednarek e di testa sigla il gol che vale il tris dei lusitani.

Martim Fernandes 6 - Spinge sicuramente meno rispetto ad Alberto Costa, presidia la sua zona di campo contro un Politano che punge poco. Perde un duello aereo con Anguissa, ma per il resto una prova sufficiente la sua. Dal 68' Sanusi 6 - Entra in una partita ormai indirizzata, non soffre dietro.

Froholdt 6 - Duello a distanza con McTominay, controllato bene dallo scozzese durante tutto il corso della sfida. Poi perde le distanze sullo scozzese nell'azione del gol degli azzurri. Cresce però nella ripresa quando il Porto domina in lungo ed in largo.

Rosario 7 - Dominatore assoluto del centrocampo, una partita perfetta per l'ex centrocampista del Nizza che non lascia nulla al caso. Corre, rincorre, recupera. In ogni zona del campo, Meret gli nega la gioia del gol.

Gabri Veiga 7,5 E' la sua partita, lo spagnolo nel 2023 era stato ad un passo dal Napoli prima di scegliere l'Arabia. Numero dieci sulle spalle, testa alta, qualità e l'assist vincente per Bednarek. La traversa colpita nella ripresa fa tremare lo stadio, poi lo fa esplodere con la rete del 2-1. Dal 78' Hwang sv.

Pepé 6,5 - La prima emozione di serata porta la sua firma, scaraventa sotto la traversa dopo pochi minuti ma viene annullato per fuorigioco. Sempre un grande apporto lungo la corsia destra. Dal 78' Fofana sv.

André Silva 5,5 - In attesa del miglior Samu, Farioli sceglie di mandarlo in campo dall'inizio. Tiene qualche buon pallone spalle alla porta, ma non punge praticamente mai davanti. Contenuto da Rrahmani. Dal 57' Samu 6,5 - Entra con la voglia di metterci la firma, ci prova in più occasioni e trova la gloria nel finale. Procura il rigore, il rosso di Rafa Marin mandando alle spalle di Meret.

Borja Sainz 6 - Gioca una buona partita, senza grandi picchi ma sempre sul pezzo quando chiamato in causa. E' la sua la prima vera occasione da rete, poi il Porto predilige la corsia opposta. Dal 68' Gimenez 6,5 - Ci prova ma non trova il gol, poi si trasveste da assistman e manda in porta Gabri Veiga.

Allenatore: Farioli 7 - Desiderava importanti risposte e soprattutto i primi punti in Champions League, approfitta di un Napoli incerottato e vive una grande serata. Quattro gol, mentalità europea e grandi doti atletiche. Poi con un Gabri Veiga così diventa tutto più facile per i lusitani.

Meret 6,5 - Avrà sospirato, temendo l'assalto, una volta visto l'approccio dei suoi. Pensiero sbagliato, ma le sue parate servono comunque: fondamentale quella a inizio secondo tempo su Rosario. Quando viene battuto, è senza colpe.

Beukema 5 - Meglio da braccetto di destra che in altre zone, avrebbe anche lo spazio per provare qualche sortita in avanti. Poi però Veiga aggredisce proprio lo spazio alle sue spalle e si invola tutto solo verso il sorpasso.

Rrahmani 5,5 - Dei tre centrali scelti per forza di cose da Allegri (non c'erano alternative a disposizione) è quello che se la cava meglio. Forse una sufficienza è un po' troppo, ma non ha espresso macchie vistose.

Marin 4,5 - Non avrebbe neanche demeritato troppo, almeno fino agli ultimi minuti. Decide di spendere un fallo abbastanza inutile su Samu, vedendosi espulso e mandando comunque il Porto su rigore: arriverà il 4-1.

Politano 5,5 - Costretto a fare le due fasi dal ruolo, mentalmente per lui non è un problema. Questo però lo priva di energie nella metà campo in cui dovrebbe e potrebbe fare la differenza, quella d'attacco. Fuori prima della fine. Dall'82' Lucca sv.

Anguissa 5,5 - Pare proprio aver perso lo smalto dei giorni migliori, anche sul piano fisico sembra solo il lontano parente di quel centrocampista protagonista di due scudetti vinti. Allegri gli risparmia qualche minuto nel finale. Dal 78' Giovane sv.

Gilmour 5,5 - Lo scozzese prova a dare i giusti tempi all'uscita del pallone, ma non è proprio la serata giusta per farlo. O comunque per riuscirci. Gli azzurri, lui compreso, alla lunga finiscono travolti dall'onda del Porto.

McTominay 6,5 - Non sta vivendo il miglior momento da quando è a Napoli, ma rimane un fattore in zona gol: l'1-1 è il terzo centro in questa Champions League. Il resto della sua prestazione al Do Dragao, onestamente, racconta poco.

Vergara 5 - Tutto portava all'adattamento di Lang, invece tocca a lui reinventarsi quinto a sinistra. Ammonito già dopo 54 secondi, destino vuole anche che Bednarek svetti proprio su di lui. Ci prova, ma è un pesce fuor d'acqua. Dal 78' Lang sv.

De Bruyne 6 - Lo si pensava in panchina, invece parte dal 1' in un ruolo più avanzato, a rimorchio di Hojlund. Piuttosto vivace, il buon segno lo si ritrova nella condizione fisica: anche negli ultimi minuti, continuava a spingere.

Hojlund 6 - La febbre è passata e rieccolo a guidare il Napoli dal centro dell'attacco. È lui a dare il segnale di risveglio ai suoi dopo la mezz'ora: in quella fase di match meritava il gol. Nella ripresa, però, sparisce.

Allenatore: Allegri 5 - Il Napoli si presenta al Do Dragao praticamente ai minimi termini: in panchina ha solo 7 giocatori, dei quali solo tre non sono portieri o giovani della Primavera. Decide di operare l'adattamento di Vergara a quinto e di avanzare la posizione di De Bruyne: l'inizio azzurro fa temere l'imbarcata, invece arriva la reazione, guidata dal recuperato Hojlund. Nel secondo tempo, però, complice anche la stanchezza fisiologica, arriva il calo complessivo e non ha neppure troppo modo di intervenire. La responsabilità da dividere anche con Manna.

Which Custom Chevy Is Your Favorite? This SEMA Pair Has the Crowd Voting

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Granatelli Motorsports, Arizona Custom Car Design, and Dixxon Flannel Co. teamed up on a C10 pickup and a K5 Blazer.

SEMA is known for ambitious collaborations and custom builds. This year, Granatelli Motorsports, Arizona Custom Car Design, and Dixxon Flannel Co. brought two brightly colored 1973 Chevrolets to the show: “Chasing Gold,” a C10 pickup, and “Sweet Emotion,” a K5 Blazer.

The unveiling marked the first time either car had been seen outside the shop, and the first time the commissioning couple, who were in attendance, had seen the finished builds. Attendees could also vote for their favorite. As of this story’s publication, “his” truck, “Chasing Gold,” is leading with 53 percent of the votes.

“Chasing Gold” is powered by a 427 LS V-8 with a 2.9-liter Whipple supercharger. A Bowler 4L80E transmission sends power through a GSI chassis to a Currie 9-inch rear end. Auto Metal Direct sheet metal and custom fabrication from Arizona Custom Car Design shaped the squarebody of the truck, while Kolor Koalition developed its custom blue paint. The front grille features custom “His” badging.

See all results for used cars for sale near 07094

“Sweet Emotion” takes a different approach, with a Stage 2 LS3 V-8 topped by a Granatelli Motorsports Zilla intake manifold. It rides on a Roadster Shop chassis and also uses a Bowler 4L80E transmission and Currie 9-inch rear end. Arizona Custom Car Design handled the bodywork and collaborated with Kolor Koalition on the Blazer’s purple finish. Of course, the custom “hers” badging is on the front of this one.

Both Chevrolets have gold trim, Variant wheels, Dakota Digital instrumentation, and Rockford Fosgate audio systems. Apex Leather supplied leather for both vehicles. Arizona Custom Car Design built the C10’s interior, while Ciadella Interiors completed the K5’s cabin. Their flannel company, Dixxon Flannel Co., is branded throughout the cabin, including their custom flannel on the seats and logo on the dashboards.

The pair brings together a long list of aftermarket companies, but the builds share a clear theme: classic Chevrolet squarebody design, updated with LS power and modern components.

Katherine Keeler is Associate Testing Editor at Hearst Autos. By day she evaluates tools for your enjoyment; by night, she Frankensteins her ever-changing fleet of rustbucket oddities back to repair. Her dream is to open a roadside attraction where the public can view, drive, and learn repairs at her emporium of curious cars.

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

One of the main reasons Baldur's Gate 3 is so beloved is because of its eclectic cast of characters. Astarion is a snarky, charismatic vampire, Shadowheart is a tough religious nut, Lae'zel is a fierce warrior, and Gale is a bit of a wet wizard. Their journey with your created Tav is special; each playthrough feels different because of their interactions.

Of course, if you don't opt to create a Tav, you can use one of Baldur's Gate 3's main cast members as an Origin Character, completing a playthrough from their perspective.

Now, a new batch of content officially vetted, uploaded, and approved by Larian is headlined by major changes to Origin Characters, giving them a brand-new perk. This comes alongside a huge new class and Warlock subclass.

Larian has done a fantastic job at keeping Baldur's Gate 3 alive. While the studio didn't really need to do anything, given just how varied each playthrough feels, it released eight major patches, with the last coming in April 2025. Although official content stopped coming, the team continued to vet the best user-created mods, uploading the cream of the crop to the console versions of its game.

This year has seen some stellar content move from PC to consoles, including a version of Shadow of Mordor's Nemesis System, a werewolf version of Shadowheart, and quite literally dozens of new races, classes, and subclasses.

Today's drop is Durge-heavy, but it's a great one. The new class and subclass are a lot of fun, and the Unnerfed Origins completely changes how Origin Characters play. It's definitely time to jump back in.

5 Tips To Make Sure You Have A Fun Time With Castlevania: Belmont’s Curse

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The great thing about Castlevania: Belmont’s Curse is that it’s a rat’s nest of puzzles, hidden rooms, and platforming challenges that’s actually enjoyable to sit down and untangle. There’s no puzzle or secret so esoteric as to be utterly confounding, but nor is it too simple. Pay enough attention, and you’ll never need a guide to get you through. However! We all could use a little help now and again to traverse the game’s rougher edges. I’ve got five tips for you that’ll make your time with Belmont’s Curse that much more enjoyable.

Through gameplay settings, developer Evil Empire has built into the game the ability for anyone, regardless of skill level, to enjoy it. And while I understand that for a very specific kind of gamer, the use of such settings is anathema, they really can be a boon. You don’t have to simply turn off deathâwhich you can doâyou can also tweak how much damage Rose takes or deals out. Increasing Rose’s experience gains can make her get stronger, faster if combat is an issue. You can choose the option to respawn outside of a boss door to cut down on runback fatigue. If traps like spike pits are messing you up as you try to tackle the game’s tricky platforming challenges, you can adjust how much damage you take from them. Even adjusting your standard allotment of healing potions can be enough to offset any difficulties you might have without bruising the ego too much.

There is so much communicated with your map beyond simply where you are in the world. Paying attention to a room’s color can let you know if you’re missing a secretâa brown background represents a completed room, a purple one still has something not yet discovered. In places like Dracula’s Castle and Notre Dame, the map will render important rooms with specific detail and can provide puzzle hints if you pay close attention. For example, it’ll tell you how many chains you need to break for the Sage and the general direction of where to find them. Use those map markers, too. You get 50 of them to place anywhere you want to make it easy to backtrack or mark things you haven’t discovered a use for yet.

Belmont’s Curse is stuffed with unique weapons, and among the seven types of armaments is bound to be one that’ll suit your play style. But while some of the weapons you find pack a decent punch, Rose’s tarot cards are where your true offensive and defensive capabilities are unlocked. Each boss you defeat grants you a new spell and, if you complete the little side-quests called Works of Mercy associated with each one, you can make them stronger. Upgrading your spells via Works of Mercy is akin to unlocking easy mode. The spells are so damn powerful that, when combined with a high-enough level for Rose, you can make many boss fights trivial. My favorite is the spell earned from the Brittle King. Fully upgrading it not only makes Rose nigh unkillable, it also adds a bit of extra damage oomph that has saved my ass in combat arenas more than once. And! A lot of the Works are simple enough to complete. Killing 25 haunted armor enemies is one Work of Mercy you can complete for the Brittle King, and you’ve already done that several times over in the time it takes to get to him.

I’ve already mentioned this elsewhere, but the mirrors are more than just save points and the game isn’t the greatest about telling you that. This is another one of those “pay close attention for a neat reward” things, but kneeling in front of one (after unlocking Dracula’s Castle) will unlock fast travel. Kneeling teleports you to a hidden area home to (almost) all of the mirrors you’ve unlocked in an area. Pick where you want to go and the mirror will get you there. The mirror world itself is a puzzle to be solved so I won’t spoil more, but until you’ve solved it, fast travel only works within a zone. You’ll have to rely on the Ferryman to travel between them.

This one is highly specific, but it is helpful for the middle of the game. After unlocking Dracula’s Castle, the next area you’ll delve into is Notre Dame. There, the final boss room is blocked off by three different lasers that you shut down one by one. There are also tons of secrets and challenges that make use of the lasers to increase their difficulty. Instead of trying to juggle laser beams sniping at you as you go for a chest hidden at the end of a dangerous room, focus on defeating the boss as soon as possible, then go back and try the challenges you missed in the old church. Turning off the lasers guarding the boss door turns them off everywhere else, making a lot of Notre Dame’s platforming challenges much easier.

Trump declares 'American space superiority for all eternity' in election night proclamation

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"The nation that leads in space will shape the future of humanity."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

President Trump issued a declaration yesterday (Nov. 3), proclaiming space open to all humanity for exploration, and the right of American citizens to develop and settle the solar system according to United States laws.

"The American Declaration of Freedom in Space," describes space as the "province of all humans" and asserts that the United States will lead humanity's expansion into the cosmos. The proclamation reiterates the administration's goals with NASA's Artemis program to return American astronauts to the moon by 2028 and eventually sending astronauts to Mars, but introduces no new funding, programs or policy directives.

"Our Nation will lead the way in the exploration, development, use, and settlement of these new frontiers — forging an interplanetary civilization founded on the enduring and inalienable rights of freedom and individual liberty," the document says.

Trump invoked America's "frontier spirit," highlighting Apollo 11 astronauts as the first people in history to walk on the moon's surface and robotic rovers exploring Mars as examples of U.S. leadership, and emphasized the country's flourishing commercial space sector helping ensure "American space superiority for all eternity."

"The nation that leads in space will shape the future of humanity," the declaration says, and pledges that America will carry the values of faith, freedom and individual liberty into an interplanetary civilization.

The proclamation also highlights the success of NASA's Artemis II mission in April, which carried four astronauts on a 10-day journey around the moon and set a record for the farthest humans have ever traveled from Earth. The follow-up to that mission, Artemis III, is scheduled to launch next summer (2027), and will test rendezvous and docking operations between NASA's Orion spacecraft and the program's two commercial lunar landers in low Earth orbit.

Artemis IV will be the program's first lunar landing mission, and is targeted for 2028. A December 2025 executive order from the President reaffirmed the goal of meeting that timeline, as well as the deployment of the initial elements for a permanent lunar outpost by 2030.

Trump's declaration yesterday does not establish new NASA programs, allocate additional funding or issue instructions to federal agencies. Its sweeping language, though, does echo some of the principles in the 1967 Outer Space Treaty, which guarantees freedom of exploration while prohibiting national sovereignty claims on other planets.

Josh Dinner is Space.com's Spaceflight Staff Writer and a photographer with a passion for science and exploration. He has worked the space beat since 2016, covering the evolution of NASA's commercial spaceflight partnerships and crewed missions, planetary science missions, the Artemis program and humanity's return to the moon. He also enjoys building 1:144-scale model rockets and spacecraft. Find some of Josh's launch photography on Instagram, and follow him on X, where he mostly posts in haiku.

Lili Reinhart, Danny Ramirez, Suki Waterhouse, Mark Duplass and Lukas Gage Team for Horror Movie ‘Violet’ From ‘Verity’ Writer (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Patrick Brice is directing the film, being produced by SK Global and Generator Entertainment and launching at the AFM with FilmNation

Lili Reinhart, Danny Ramirez, Suki Waterhouse, Mark Duplass and Lukas Gage are teaming for “Violet,” a psychosexual horror that looks likely to be one of the hotter packages going into the American Film Market.

The film is being directed by acclaimed genre filmmaker Patrick Brice, who is behind the “Creep” franchise. It was written by Nick Antosca, fresh of the back of his box office hit “Verity,” and Alana B. Lytle, who adapted from Antosca’s short story “Ghostfish.” SK Global and Generator Entertainment are producing and financing the film. Related Stories Netflix U.K. Layoffs Hit Up to 3% of London HQ, Production VP Anna Mallett Exits 'The Diplomat,' 'Nobody Wants This,' 'East of Eden' Among Series to Take Part in Netflix's 'FYSee: The Fall Edit' Awards Showcase (EXCLUSIVE)

FilmNation Entertainment will launch sales, both domestic and international, on “Violet” at the AFM, with production slated to begin in spring 2027.

The synopsis reads: Two years after the sudden death of his fiancée, Violet (Waterhouse), Sean (Ramirez) has finally begun to move on, finding love with his childhood friend Kate (Reinhart). But when the couple moves into the home Sean once shared with Violet, he starts receiving unsettling text messages from someone, or something, claiming to be his dead ex. In an age of constant connectivity— where every text, photo, and digital footprint lingers forever— do exes ever really let you go?

In addition to SK Global and Generator Entertainment financing the film, Kelly McKee and Kenneth Browning will produce for Generator Entertainment, alongside Elizabeth Haggard and Dylan Tarason for SK Global, and Antosca and Alex Hedlund for Eat the Cat. Executive producers include Jeannine Tang, Chris Collins, Rasool Alizadeh, Gemma Levinson, and Jackson Stewart. Emmy nominee Jessica Kelly (“Euphoria,” “A Real Pain,” “Pieces of a Woman”) is serving as casting director.

Alongside the horror franchise “Creep,” which he co-wrote with Duplass (who also starred), Brice’s other directing credits include the Sundance competition film “The Overnight” starring Adam Scott, “Corporate Animals” starring Demi Moore, and the Netflix horror film “There’s Someone Inside Your House.” He also co-created, co-wrote, and directed the Shudder series “The Creep Tapes.”

Antosca, meanwhile, recently wrote the Colleen Hoover adaptation “Verity,” starring Anne Hathaway and Dakota Johnson, which earned more than $170 million globally earlier this year. He’s also known for creating the horror anthology series “Channel Zero” and Hulu’s Emmy and Golden Globe-winning limited series “The Act,” plus “Brand New Cherry Flavor,” “A Friend of the Family” and Apple TV’s recent adaptation of “Cape Fear.” He also wrote the supernatural horror film “Antlers,” based on his short story “The Quiet Boy.”

“Riverdale” breakout Reinhart most recently starred in Amazon’s acclaimed “The Love Hypothesis,” while she can also be seen in “Look Both Ways” for Netflix and “Hustlers.”

Ramirez’s credits include the global hits “Top Gun: Maverick” and “Captain America: Brave New World,” while he’s slated to star in Todd Haynes’ “De Noche” opposite Pedro Pascal, the Russo Brothers’ “Avengers: Doomsday” and Joseph Kosinski’s “Miami Vice ’85.”

Waterhouse, best known for “Daisy Jones & The Six,” “Assassination Nation” and “Love Rosie,” recently released her third studio album “Loveland” via Island Records.

Multi-hyphenate Duplass, an Emmy winner for exec producing the Netflix docuseries “Wild Wild Country” and two time Emmy nominee for his acting in “The Morning Show,” was recently seen in global smash hit “Backrooms,” while his other film credits include “Zero Dark Thirty” and “Bombshell.”

Gage’s film credits include hit horror sequel “Smile 2,” Prime Video smash “Road House,” plus “People We Meet on Vacation,” “Companion,” TIFF hit “How to Blow Up a Pipeline” and Netflix’s “Voicemails for Isabelle.”

Brice is represented by CAA, Untitled, and Jackoway Austen Tyerman; Antosca by WME and Ginsburg Daniels Kallis; Reinhart by UTA, Entertainment 360, and Peikoff Mahan; Ramirez by CAA, Brillstein Entertainment Partners, Entertainment 360, Viewpoint, and Hansen Jacobson Teller; Waterhouse by CAA and Independent Talent Group; Duplass by CAA and Hansen Jacobson Teller; Gage by WME and Goodman Genow Schenkman.

Winter Storm Watch vs. Winter Storm Warning: What’s the Difference?

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A winter storm watch and winter storm warning mean different things. Here's what each alert signals and how to prepare for dangerous weather.

Tracking severe weather can sometimes feel like it requires a degree in meteorology. With complex scientific terms and technical jargon, it can be difficult to understand what it all means and what should be done to prepare. That’s when the difference between a winter storm watch and a winter storm warning matters.

Both alerts come from the National Weather Service, but they call for different levels of urgency. Here’s how the alerts work and what homeowners should do when they see one.

A winter storm watch means a significant winter storm is possible, but forecasters aren’t yet sure where or when it will hit or how severe it will be. According to the National Weather Service, watches often arrive a day or two before a storm and can cover heavy snow, sleet, or ice.

They give residents time to prepare, even though the storm may not reach their area. As the forecast becomes clearer, the National Weather Service may upgrade the watch to a warning, issue an advisory, or cancel it altogether.

A winter storm warning means dangerous winter weather is expected or already happening. Heavy snow, sleet, or ice could make travel unsafe and cause other problems, including power outages. The amount of snow or ice needed to trigger a warning varies by location.

“Each office knows the local area and will issue Warnings, Watches or Advisories based on local criteria,” according to the National Weather Service. “For example, the amount of snow that triggers a ‘Winter Storm Warning’ in the Northern Plains is typically much higher than the amount needed to trigger a ‘Winter Storm Warning’ in the Southeast.”

Warnings generally arrive closer to a storm’s arrival than watches, although forecasters can issue one before snow or ice begins falling.

A winter weather advisory means snow, sleet, or ice is expected to cause problems, but forecasters don’t anticipate conditions severe enough for a warning. An advisory may signal slick roads or other travel disruptions. While the weather may be less severe than during a warning, residents should still take precautions and monitor local forecasts.

A winter storm watch gives homeowners time to prepare before conditions worsen. That includes checking heating systems, protecting exposed pipes from freezing, and stocking up on food, medications, and other essentials.

“Your primary concerns at home or work during a winter storm are loss of heat, power and telephone service and a shortage of supplies if storm conditions continue for more than a day,” according to the National Weather Service.

Homeowners should have flashlights, spare batteries, and a charged phone in case the power goes out. Checking smoke and carbon monoxide alarms, locating snow shovels, and stocking up on ice melt can also help.

Once a warning takes effect, homeowners should finish their preparations and avoid unnecessary travel. Staying indoors is generally safest during severe winter weather.

If the power goes out, residents can close off unused rooms and wear extra layers to stay warm. Portable generators should always remain outdoors, away from windows and doors. Grills and ovens should never serve as heating sources because they can create serious safety hazards.

Residents should continue monitoring weather alerts throughout the storm. Once conditions improve, homeowners can check for damage and begin clearing snow and ice from walkways and driveways.

National Weather Service, “Winter Weather Warnings, Watches and Advisories” National Weather Service, “Winter Weather Resources and Frequently Asked Questions” National Weather Service, “Prepare! Don’t Let a Winter Storm Take You by Surprise” Federal Emergency Management Agency, “Be Prepared for a Winter Storm”

Almost no one uses AI agents to book their Uber, company says

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Very few people are using personal AI agents to order Ubers, apparently. On the transportation giant’s third-quarter earnings call on Wednesday, the company said that only four rides were ordered through an AI agent during its busiest week in October: the week of Halloween.

While more people used an AI agent to order food through its delivery service, Uber Eats, the volume was still fairly small compared to the overall number of trips.

Uber’s chief financial officer, Balaji Krishnamurthy, told Wall Street analysts on the call that the company handled around 320 million trips and orders across Uber and Uber Eats during the week of Halloween, but only 500 Uber Eats orders were placed by an AI agent.

“It is very, very early and very limited traction that we are seeing so far,” said CEO Dara Khosrowshahi, referring to the low number of orders placed by AI agents. “Listen, we hope that there will be more traction.”

The surprisingly low numbers of AI-initiated transactions come amid growing hype for a new wave of AI agents that don’t just answer questions but also handle tasks on users’ behalf. There’s been an explosion of personal AI startups, including one of the buzziest, Instinct, which was founded last year and raised its most recent round of funding at a $10 billion valuation. Meta’s Muse also became the No. 1 app in Apple’s U.S. App Store last month, topping 10 million users, per Meta’s own reporting. The company said many were using it to shop and transact with businesses.

OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and SpaceXAI (Grok) are also now offering their own AI agents, as are numerous smaller startups.

Still, early numbers from public companies indicate the agentic web still has a long way to go. DoorDash, for instance, said last week on its earnings call that it has only received a few hundred orders from AI agents so far, as most customers are still placing orders through the DoorDash app.

Uber is seeing the same behavior, it seems. Khosrowshahi pointed out that the average time to book a ride is only 50 seconds in the company’s app, and re-ordering a favorite food only takes 90 seconds. That suggests an AI agent isn’t necessarily saving anyone time.

Despite the low number of AI-driven transactions, Khosrowshahi seemed bullish on the idea of agents in general. “We think that is another opportunity to reach our platform. We think it will be a net positive in terms of demand on the ecosystem,” he said.

Right now, there are around eight major AI assistants on the market, Khosrowshahi noted, and he expects that number to grow to more than 10 in time.

“Every single AI system company is talking to us,” he added.

Certinia brings AI into professional services workflows

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Certinia Inc. is bringing AI into the software firms use to sell projects, assign staff, deliver work and bill clients.

AI’s ability to instantaneously draw from a deep well of information and provide guidance in practically any field of expertise has changed the paradigm for professional services. For Certinia Inc., whose business facilitates 7.1 billion professional services operations per month, AI represents a significant opportunity for its clients.

“I think the only industry that’s probably been more disrupted by AI than the technology industry has probably been the services industry,” Robert Cesafsky, chief operating officer of Certinia, told theCUBE. “Whether you are a software company, a hardware company, a tax and accounting firm [or] management consulting … all of these industries are being directly targeted or impacted by AI. Whatever your strategy is, you need to be able to have the right tools. You need to be able to have control of that services business, and that’s what Certinia provides.”

This feature is part of SiliconANGLE Media’s exploration of the architectural shifts powering continuous, production-grade AI. Be sure to check out theCUBE’s coverage of the Certinia at Dreamforce event. (* Disclosure below.)

Certinia launched in 2009 as FinancialForce.com Inc., a cloud-based accounting and enterprise resource planning provider built natively on the Salesforce Inc. platform. It rebranded in 2023 as it broadened from accounting and ERP to managing services businesses.

Certinia’s Salesforce-native platform connects a wide range of processes spanning sales, project delivery, financial management and customer success. The company’s approach helps clients transform cost centers into profit centers by improving visibility into customer activities, optimizing resource deployment and maintaining connectivity throughout the customer lifecycle.

“We can take away all the administrative tasks and outsource that to AI in order to help them deliver faster time outcomes, but it also helps them deliver a lower cost to serve that project,” said Mimi Spier, chief marketing officer of Certinia, in an interview with theCUBE. “We can identify expansion opportunities, [and] at-risk customers where we need to optimize renewals. Everything we’re doing is ultimately affecting the bottom line.”

Certinia’s long-time relationship with Salesforce is a key element in the company’s overall strategy. Certinia is featured under the AI agents and tools category for Salesforce’s AgentExchange and has provided AI-powered sales and delivery for Salesforce’s consulting partner network.

“We have a deep partnership with Salesforce,” said Raju Malhotra, chief product and technology officer of Certinia. “I spend personally a lot of time with the Salesforce executives on business and engineering, and we collaborate. We bring our customer use cases, they tell us about what’s coming up from a capability perspective and we work very well together. They’re led by a founder [chief executive officer] who’s very much in tune with the market, and they want to stay ahead.”

Certinia has sought to stay ahead in its own right by introducing Veda, an enterprise-grade intelligent operations engine that functions as the company’s suite of AI specialist agents and intelligent actions. The solution delivers rules-bound, trusted and ROI-focused autonomous workflows, combining Certinia’s years of institutional memory with advanced AI reasoning. Certinia’s goal with Veda is to provide customers with enough flexibility so that agents integrated into existing business workflows can drive immediate and measurable value.

“We’ve infused Veda throughout the experience for users to be more productive,” Malhotra said. “We have package agents, which are Veda agents. We launched Veda with 10 packages. Now we have 24, and we will finish the year with more than 40 package agents. Those agents are very much persona based, and they help our customers.”

Veda is not simply a layer of generic automation; it represents a key shift in how AI is being used in the enterprise. Certinia’s focus on serving the professional services market motivated the company to ground its AI offering in operational reality, where context, coordination and control matter as much as speed, according to Scott Hebner, principal AI analyst at theCUBE Research.

“Certinia’s recent announcement of Veda is more profound than it may appear on the surface,” he said. “Veda is notable not simply because it introduces a new suite of AI agents and intelligent workflow-based actions, but because it offers a glimpse into what the future of enterprise AI is likely to look like: AI that works alongside humans in consequential workflows, helps people make better decisions and earns trust by being grounded in business context, governance and real operational outcomes.”

Since the launch of Veda in April, Certinia has taken several actions to enhance its AI capabilities. In July, the firm acquired Moonnox, an AI-native services delivery platform purpose-built for the professional services sector.

This acquisition added a contextual layer to Veda through knowledge graphs, persistent project memory and services-specific agents. The combined platform can now connect a new client request with approved scope, staffing requirements and financial impact, then recommend governed actions for professional review, according to Hebner.

“Certinia provides the deep, structured foundation of professional services, customer success and financial management; Moonnox brings the missing half: capturing the unstructured reality — the emails, chats, and calls — where delivery actually happens,” he explained. “Together, they fuse everything a services business knows with everything their teams do, moving Veda beyond orchestration toward context-aware, AI-native services delivery.”

The context graph can also draw on structured records such as timecard entries and utilization figures. Moonnox helps bring that information into the graph for client-facing teams managing projects, according to Cesafsky.

“That’s been where Moonnox has really showed up for us over the last couple of months,” he said. “It’s those capabilities to help the client-facing services [and] delivery teams run and operate their projects. [Moonnox] is effectively an intake machine for a lot of that structured data to bring that into the context graph as well.”

In September, Certinia unveiled its System of Action for services businesses. The offering significantly expanded the Veda AI suite with new agents and more than doubled the Veda Intelligent Actions library from 64 to 135.

Certinia’s System of Action uses a three-tier architecture to bring AI agents into services workflows. The first tier brings together structured records and unstructured project data. The second draws on more than 17 years of Certinia’s experience in services to inform decisions. The third lets agents act within existing workflows.

“We want to accelerate the delivery for our customers,” Malhotra said. “Our customers are solving some specific problems in the professional services world. We don’t want to just give you a project plan. If you choose to execute that project plan with some agents, we’ll give you that. If you want to really think about agentic capabilities to help you in making money, in expanding your time [and] in terms of delivery, we will help you with that.”

For professional services firms, adopting AI agents means deciding which parts of project delivery to delegate to them.

“We view professional services as a leading indicator of where enterprise AI is heading because it combines knowledge-intensive work, dynamic context, collaborative decisions, measurable economics and human accountability,” Hebner said. “If Certinia executes well, Veda could become an important blueprint for translating digital labor into economic outcomes.”

‘90210’ Alums Tori Spelling & Brian Austin Green To Star In Vertical Microdrama For VertTV

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tori Spelling and Brian Austin Green have been tapped to star in a new vertical microdrama for vertTV.

The Beverly Hills, 90210 stars will reunite in the vertical series, which recently wrapped production in Los Angeles.

Carol Potter, who played Cindy Walsh, the mom of twins Brenda and Brandon Walsh in the Fox series, will also be featured in the microdrama.

Plot details are being kept under wraps for the vertTV thriller. Other shows on the vertical streaming app include Finding Hope (reuniting Fuller House favorites Jodie Sweetin, John Brotherton, and Marla Sokoloff alongside Richard Karn) and Chonda (starring Chonda Pierce and Regan Burns), which broke ground as the vertical microseries format’s first multicam sitcom.

Based in Los Angeles, vertTV continues to elevate mobile entertainment by pairing traditional L.A. production infrastructure, experienced local crews, and veteran Hollywood screenwriters with fast-paced vertical storytelling. Powered by human intelligence, the studio remains deeply committed to authentic artistry, shooting every series with real actors on real sets without the use of generative AI. Watch on Deadline

Vertical microdramas continue to gain traction as they meet viewers where they spend most of their time on — mobile phones. Peacock recently announced the premiere of Bravo’s first scripted microdrama titled Charmed by a Southern Monster, starring Craig Conover, star of the reality series Southern Charm.

The Bravo Original scripted vertical microdrama premieres Monday, November 9, on the Peacock mobile app, with Nicole Mattox co-starring alongside Conover. In Charmed by a Southern Monster, Conover plays Hunter Caldwell while Mattox plays Maeve Marchant. The series blends soapy romance, workplace rivalry, and small-town mystery.
